# The site in plain words: what was changed on 11 October 2026

*Written 2026-10-12T23:35:54Z by `scripts/write_site_copy.py`. No figure in this document was typed: each count, time, hash and
sentence of the page was read by that script from the files listed at the end, which it holds against the builds on
disk before it writes a word.*

## 1. What this is

On 11 October 2026 the owner asked for three things to be put right on the map page that his supervisor and the
Foundation for Ecological Security will open, without moving a single figure:

- **some of it was no longer true**: NIRv was shown as pending, the page called itself offline, and more (section 2);
- **it read as if a machine had written it**: long labels, a question for a switch. A label is now short, and what
  it left out stands in a small "i" beside it, which opens a bubble on hover and on keyboard focus, and on a click,
  which is what a tap on a touch screen sends (no touch screen was tried);
- **the published copy showed an overlay box that could not work in it.** By his ruling of that day the box is now
  greyed out in the copy and says so.

The words are his: `2026-10-11_site_copy_deck.md`, beside the handoff among the session's working files. Where a word
of his would have misstated something it was changed, and section 11 lists each such change.

This is the account of that work, for the two builds as they stand: the full layer in `60_layer/`, built
2026-10-12T22:00:54Z, and the copy made to be published, `70_share/odisha_forest_decision_layer/`, made 2026-10-12T22:01:24Z from it.
Publishing the copy is the owner's step and is not part of this document; `docs/DECISION_LAYER.md` says what the site
serves.

**How it was checked, in one paragraph.** Before a word was changed both builds were copied aside with a list of
their hashes. `scripts/check_site_copy.py` holds the builds against those copies: 59 checks, all passed
(section 9), among them that `60_layer/layer_by_unit.csv` is byte for byte what it was, that no part of either
`data.js` differs but the parts that hold words, and that every unit's row is what it was but for one reworded
warning. 35 errors were planted on copies and each was caught (section 9). The layer's own checks were run
again in Chrome and in WebKit with their expected words brought up to date, no expected number changed
(section 8). And every "i" was opened in a browser at three window sizes and looked at (section 10).

**What one data key was added.** `data.js` gained one key at its top, `tips`, which holds the words of every "i".
Nothing else was added to the data that day. (On 13 October 2026 a second key was added, `guide_html`, the guide that
the page's third button opens: section 14.)

**Six lines were corrected later the same day.** The audit had listed them for the owner with nothing changed; in his
third handoff of 11 October 2026 he gave his words for each and asked that every other string stay as it is. They
are the last 7 rows of section 2, with his ruling, and section 13 sets each string beside what it was.

**A Guide button and a loader were added on 13 October 2026.** A third button opens the owner's guide to the map,
with its pictures, in the window the other two texts use; and the map's area says what the page is doing while it
opens. No word that this document describes was changed by it. Its account is section 14: what was added, the
pictures, and the gate that traces every figure of the guide.

that stood in section 3, listed for the owner, until he gave his words for them in his third handoff of the day

## 3. Listed for the owner, with nothing changed

17 findings are listed and nothing was changed for them. Each is one of these: a string that comes from
`55_decision/` (a cell's name or reason, a tier's reason, a work-type field, a window's description, the governance
prompt); a sentence of `docs/RESULTS.md`, or of another document this task does not rewrite; words the deck keeps as
they are; or the owner's own words. `scripts/check_site_copy.py` holds that each of the page's is still on the page as
quoted here. The lines he has since reworded are no longer here: they are in section 2.

| layers[agreement].this_shows | legend note | This shows, for units with at least 100 ha of land with canopy above 10 % in 2000, whether each measure ranks the unit in the top fifth of such units. Where both do, the claim that the unit degraded is as strong as these two sources can make it. Where one does, neither is preferred. It is about units, not hectares: the two have not been overlaid pixel by pixel. Units wholly under CoRE Stack's East Coast zone model are left out of this comparison and drawn as a class of their own, because CoRE Stack's own check fails there (see 'What this is'); their panel gives the class they would have with nothing left out. | Units with at least 100 ha of land with canopy above 10 % in 2000, by whether each measure puts them in its top fifth. | reworded |
| layers[allocation].this_shows | legend note | This shows what was spent on works whose recorded location falls inside the unit - not what the panchayat of record received: only 65 works in 100 lie in the unit that holds most of their own panchayat's works. The record effectively ends with works started in 2017-18. | reworded |
| layers[forest_named_expenditure].this_shows | legend note | This shows spending on works typed as afforestation, forest protection, eco-restoration of forest or forestry plantation: 1.1 % of all expenditure on the works in these units. The wider 'Plantation' category (15 %) includes orchards and is given in the panel for each unit. | Works typed as afforestation, forest protection, eco-restoration of forest or forestry plantation: 1.1 % of all expenditure on the works in these units. | reworded |
| layers[st_share].this_shows | legend note | This shows the share of each unit's 2011 population that is Scheduled Tribe. The first two breaks, 18 % and 33 %, are the ones CoRE Stack's Know Your Landscape dashboard uses. | Share of each unit's 2011 population that is Scheduled Tribe. | reworded |
| layers[adi_2011].this_shows | legend note | This shows a village development index from Census 2011 - the sum of five ranks from 1 (low) to 3 (advanced): bathroom facility, cooking fuel, source of drinking water, assets, literacy - averaged over each unit's villages by population. | A village development index from Census 2011: five ranks from 1 (low) to 3 (advanced), for bathroom facility, cooking fuel, source of drinking water, assets and literacy, added up and averaged over the unit's villages by population. | reworded |
| tips.layer:nirv | i |   | We tested a Landsat 8 greenness trend (NIRv, 2013-2025) as a measure of thinning. Its result depended on how we corrected for the date of each image, and a single image could flip a pixel's flag, so we don't use it. | new: an i; the unit panel's NIRv section and the About text |
| tips.layer:forest_area | i |   | It's the land the next layer's loss is counted on. It isn't Odisha's recorded forest area, and it isn't the Forest Survey's forest cover. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:hansen_loss | i |   | This layer counts loss only where canopy was above 30 % in 2000; the next layer shows it on more open land. Hansen records clearing, tree canopy removed outright: it can't see thinning under a canopy that's still standing. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:canopy_band_loss | i |   | Of all Hansen loss in these units, 45 % is outside the forest mask; this band is the part of it that stood as trees in 2000. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:corestack_degradation | i |   | CoRE Stack marks a pixel "Degradation" when a classifier that's right about two times in three says it crossed its zone's threshold. Read it next to Improvement, which is the same noise the other way (next layer). | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:corestack_net | i |   | Both classes carry the same classifier noise, so the net is the safer reading. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:agreement | i |   | Where both do, the claim that the unit degraded is as strong as these two sources can make it; where one does, neither is preferred. It's about units, not hectares: the two haven't been overlaid pixel by pixel. Units wholly under CoRE Stack's East Coast zone model are a class of their own, because our placebo test fails there, though on few districts (see About this map); their panel gives the class they'd have with nothing left out. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:allocation | i |   | Spending on works whose recorded location falls inside the unit, not what the panchayat of record received: only 65 works in 100 lie in the unit that holds most of their own panchayat's works. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:forest_named_expenditure | i |   | The wider Plantation category (15 %) includes orchards; each unit's panel shows it. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |
| tips.layer:st_share | i |   | The first two breaks, 18 % and 33 %, are the ones CoRE Stack's Know Your Landscape dashboard uses. | new: an i; the note under this layer's legend |

### About this map


| Where | What it is | Before | After | What happened |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| about_html, block 1 | text | Forest condition beside MGNREGA allocation and social need - Odisha | About this map | reworded |
| about_html, block 2 | text | What this is. A pre-computed, offline map of 11,468 units covering Odisha: 7,976 LGD panchayat units and 3,492 outside-village units. For each unit it shows forest and its loss (Hansen), forest health and degradation (CoRE Stack), whether those two agree, MGNREGA expenditure on the works geotagged inside the unit, and Census 2011 measures. Click a unit to read its figures; choose a layer on the left. | What you're looking at. A map of 11,468 units covering Odisha: 7,976 LGD panchayat units and 3,492 outside-village units. For each unit you can see its forest and its loss (Hansen), forest health and degradation (CoRE Stack), whether those two agree, what MGNREGA spent on the works geotagged inside it, and its Census 2011 figures. Pick a layer on the left, then click a unit to see its numbers. | reworded |
| about_html, block 3 | text | Why it exists. The paper on Commons Connect (Mehta et al., ICTD 2024) describes a tool for water security built on the CoRE Stack and says: “We envision that similarly other tools can be built, for example, for agroforestry planning, forest conservation and restoration, tracking of carbon credits, etc. by suitably bringing together new datasets and methods for these new domains of ecological sustainability.” This is a first step toward such a tool for forest: datasets (tree canopy, its loss, tree-cover change), indicators per unit, and one view of them. It is a demonstration for discussion, to be checked against what people who know these places observe - not a verdict on any place. | Why we built it. The paper on Commons Connect (Mehta et al., ICTD 2024) describes a tool for water security built on the CoRE Stack and says: “We envision that similarly other tools can be built, for example, for agroforestry planning, forest conservation and restoration, tracking of carbon credits, etc. by suitably bringing together new datasets and methods for these new domains of ecological sustainability.” This map is a first step toward such a tool for forests: datasets (tree canopy, its loss, tree-cover change), indicators per unit, and one view of them. It's a demo for discussion, to be checked against what people who know these places see: not a verdict on any place. | reworded |
| about_html, block 4 | text | The unit. Commons Connect computes its landscape-level indicators per micro-watershed, and the consultation catalogue lists its forest variables per forest beat. This map uses administrative polygons instead, because MGNREGA funds and the census are administrative: an LGD panchayat unit is the villages mapped to one LGD panchayat id (not always a whole gram panchayat). Outside-village units (hatched) are the land outside any revenue village; what that land is in law is not in the data used here. They are 27 % of the area and hold 59 % of the forest and 57 % of its loss, and they have no census rows. The few blank patches inside the state are 161 villages (753 km2) that have a census code but no row in the village-to-panchayat mapping: they belong to no unit. The district of an outside-village unit is the district named on its boundary polygon, checked against the villages round it; the panel says so where it was assigned another way or is ambiguous. | What a unit is. Commons Connect computes its landscape-level indicators per micro-watershed, and the same group's report on community consultations lists forest variables per forest beat. We use administrative units instead, because MGNREGA funds and the census are administrative: an LGD panchayat unit is the villages mapped to one LGD panchayat id (not always a whole gram panchayat). Outside-village units (hatched) are the land outside any revenue village; what that land is in law isn't in the data we used. They are 27 % of the area and hold 59 % of the forest and 57 % of its loss, and they have no census rows. The few blank patches inside the state are 161 villages (753 km2) that have a census code but no row in the village-to-panchayat mapping: they belong to no unit. The district of an outside-village unit is the district named on its boundary polygon, checked where it could be against the panchayat polygons under it and the villages round it; its panel says what the district rests on, and where a check contradicts it or none was possible. | reworded |
| about_html, block 5 | text | Honest colour. More than half of the units (6,265 of 11,468) have under one hectare of forest, and a rate on a little forest is noise: between odd and even years the rank correlation of a unit's loss rate is 0.34 for units with 1 to 2 ha of forest and 0.61 at 5 to 10 ha. On the land the two measures are compared on it is still only 0.73 at 50 to 100 ha, and 0.84 at 100 to 250 ha. So on the forest layers a unit with too little of the land a layer rests on is grey - insufficient forest to assess - and never on the colour scale. The minimum is 100 ha, the floor ruled for the analysis (9,401 units grey on the Hansen layer; the coloured units still hold 97.0 % of the forest). You can set it to 50 ha or to 1 ha to see what the stricter floor hides. On the census layers the outside-village units are no census data for this land, never zero. | Small forests are greyed out. More than half of the units (6,265 of 11,468) have under one hectare of forest, and a rate on a little forest is noise: between odd and even years the rank correlation of a unit's loss rate is 0.34 for units with 1 to 2 ha of forest and 0.61 at 5 to 10 ha. On the land the two measures are compared on it is still only 0.73 at 50 to 100 ha, and 0.84 at 100 to 250 ha. So on the forest layers a unit with too little of the land a layer rests on is grey - insufficient forest to assess - and never on the colour scale. The minimum is 100 ha, the one we use in the analysis (9,401 units grey on the Hansen layer; the coloured units still hold 97.0 % of the forest). You can set it to 50 ha or 1 ha to see what the minimum hides. On the census layers the outside-village units are no census data for this land, never zero. | reworded |
| about_html, block 7 | text | Hansen tree cover loss dates the outright removal of canopy that was fairly closed in 2000. It is validated and it is a floor: it cannot see thinning, and it cannot see change on land that was already open in 2000. | Hansen tree cover loss dates the outright removal of tree canopy, year by year. It's validated, and it's a floor: it can't see thinning under a canopy that still stands. | reworded |
| about_html, block 13 | text | The first is the headline because CoRE Stack's Improvement class is the check on its Degradation class: clearance should not go with improvement. In the 468 compared units under the East Coast model it does - a rank correlation of 0.35, against 0.07 under the other model - so agreement there cannot be read as agreement about degradation. Those are also the units where the two agree most, so leaving them out lowers the figure; they are drawn as a class of their own on the agreement layer. The agreement that remains is modest, and in the LGD panchayat units taken alone it is not established: their odds ratio is 1.55 with an interval of 0.80 to 3.61, which includes 1. Intervals come from resampling whole districts. The two measures do not measure the same thing and are not expected to mark the same pixels. | The first is the headline because we use CoRE Stack's Improvement class as a check on its Degradation class, our placebo test: clearance should not go with improvement. In the 468 compared units under the East Coast model it does - a rank correlation of 0.35, against 0.07 under the other model - so agreement there cannot be read as agreement about degradation. (That failure is a strong pattern in few districts, not an established fact.) Those are also the units where the two agree most, so leaving them out lowers the figure; they are drawn as a class of their own on the agreement layer. The agreement that remains is modest, and in the LGD panchayat units taken alone it is not established: their odds ratio is 1.55 with an interval of 0.80 to 3.61, which includes 1. Intervals come from resampling whole districts. The two measures do not measure the same thing and are not expected to mark the same pixels. | reworded |
| about_html, block 14 | text | Units, not hectares. The two measures agree about which units are degrading, not yet about which hectares. A pixel-by-pixel overlap of the two has not been run: it needs one more Earth Engine job. | Units, not hectares. Where the two measures agree, they agree about which units are degrading, not which hectares: we haven't overlaid them pixel by pixel. That needs one more Earth Engine job. | reworded |
| about_html, block 15 | text | NIRv (a greenness trend from Landsat 8) is pending and will be a reported column only. | Greenness trend (NIRv): tested, not used. We tested a Landsat 8 greenness trend (NIRv, 2013-2025) as a measure of thinning. Its result depended on how we corrected for the date of each image, and a single image could flip a pixel's flag, so on 7 October 2026 we decided it isn't a measure of degradation here. The state-wide run stopped partway and nothing is queued, so it isn't on this map. | reworded |
| about_html, block 17 | text | The shapes are simplified so that the file can be e-mailed: 2,447,144 boundary points to 1,232,815, with a tolerance of 30 m and shared boundaries kept shared. The whole area changes by -0.00001 %; a unit's drawn area by 0.022 % at the median and under 0.39 % for 95 units in 100; 120 very small units (none larger than 71.1 ha) change by more than 5 %. Every area printed in a panel is the unit's true area, not that of the simplified shape. There is no background map: nothing is fetched from the internet. | The shapes are simplified so that the file can be e-mailed: 2,447,144 boundary points to 1,232,815, with a tolerance of 30 m and shared boundaries kept shared. The whole area changes by -0.00001 %; a unit's drawn area by 0.022 % at the median and under 0.39 % for 95 units in 100; 120 very small units (none larger than 71.1 ha) change by more than 5 %. Every area printed in a panel is the unit's true area, not that of the simplified shape. There's no background map, and the layers above ask no other site for anything. | reworded |
| about_html, block 18 | text | To check a figure. Every number shown is a cell of layer_by_unit.csv, which is built from the stage tables degradation_by_unit.csv, agreement_by_unit.csv, need_by_unit.csv and allocation_by_unit.csv. | To check a figure. Every number in a unit's panel is a cell of layer_by_unit.csv, which is built from the stage tables degradation_by_unit.csv, corestack_by_unit_bands.csv, agreement_by_unit.csv, need_by_unit.csv and allocation_by_unit.csv; the decision view's come from condition_by_unit.csv. The page looks figures up; the only counting it does is of the rows of a list, the matches of a search and a tier's place in the order. | reworded |
| about_html, block 19 | text | Decision view: where is forest work warranted? | The decision view | reworded |
| about_html, block 20 | text | What it is. A second way to read the same measurements, added beside the layers above; nothing in them is changed by it. Each unit is placed in one of six cells by two questions put to Hansen's record: how much forest is standing now, and is it mostly closed or mostly open; and was forest lost in the window of years you choose. The cells are put in an order of tiers, and within one district the units are listed tier by tier. Within a tier, largest first: by forest loss in the window where the cell has loss, by standing hectares of the cell's band where it has none. It is a prompt for where to look, to be set against what people who know the places see. It is not a verdict on any place, and it is not a score. | What it is. A second way to read the same measurements; it changes nothing in the layers above. Each unit is placed in one of six cells by two questions put to Hansen's record: how much forest is standing now, and is it mostly closed or mostly open; and was forest lost in the window of years you choose. The cells are put in an order of tiers, and within one district the units are listed tier by tier. Within a tier, most hectares lost first; in a cell without loss, most standing hectares of its kind first. It's a starting point for where to look, to be checked against what people who know the places see. It is not a verdict on any place, and it is not a score. | reworded |
| about_html, block 24 | text | Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate mostly open none 3,055 614 | Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate mostly open little or none 3,055 614 | reworded |
| about_html, block 25 | text | Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect mostly dense none 1,040 514 | Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect mostly dense little or none 1,040 514 | reworded |
| about_html, block 27 | text | Not a forest site little or none none 5,639 1,688 | Not a forest site little or none little or none 5,639 1,688 | reworded |
| about_html, block 30 | text | Two thresholds, ruled on by the project owner on 9 October 2026 as they were proposed. A unit has little or none under 10 ha of standing forest; it has some loss from 1 ha of forest loss in the window. Neither is the 100 ha of the agreement test, which is a floor for comparing rates and is not used here. One pixel is about 0.07 ha, and in 2018-2025 147 units have a recorded forest loss above zero and under 0.0725 ha, the area of one pixel in the middle of the state: slivers cut by a boundary and, where pixels are smaller than that, whole single pixels. A single pixel must not decide a cell; 1 ha is the smallest patch the Forest Survey of India counts as forest cover. Units with 10 ha or more standing hold 99.6 % of all standing forest. Among units with 10 ha or more standing, 88 % of those that lost 1 ha or more in 2018-2021 lost that much again in 2022-2025; of those that lost under 0.0725 ha, or nothing, 5 %. The thresholds matter. In the window 2018-2025: with 1 ha for loss, moving the standing minimum to 5 ha or 25 ha moves 990 or 1,207 units to another cell; with 10 ha standing, moving the loss minimum to 0.5 ha or 2 ha moves 308 or 282. A fixed number of hectares also follows the size of a unit's forest: 5 % of the forest sites with 10 to 25 ha standing have some loss, and 85 % of those with 5,000 ha or more; 349 of the 1,295 units in Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first lost under 1 % of the forest they had at the start of the window, and 74 forest sites in a cell with little or no loss recorded lost over 2 % of theirs. That is why each unit's loss is also shown as a share (below). And 173 units have under 10 ha of land and so have little or none whatever their cover: 170 of them are in Not a forest site and 3, which lost 1 ha or more in the window, in Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground. In 37 units of the cell Not a forest site standing forest covers half or more of the land. | Two thresholds, ruled on 9 October 2026 as they were proposed. A unit has little or none under 10 ha of standing forest; it has some loss from 1 ha of forest loss in the window. Neither is the 100 ha of the agreement test, which is a floor for comparing rates and is not used here. One pixel is about 0.07 ha, and in 2018-2025 147 units have a recorded forest loss above zero and under 0.0725 ha, the area of one pixel in the middle of the state: slivers cut by a boundary and, where pixels are smaller than that, whole single pixels. A single pixel must not decide a cell; 1 ha is the smallest patch the Forest Survey of India counts as forest cover. Units with 10 ha or more standing hold 99.6 % of all standing forest. Among units with 10 ha or more standing, 88 % of those that lost 1 ha or more in 2018-2021 lost that much again in 2022-2025; of those that lost under 0.0725 ha, or nothing, 5 %. The thresholds matter. In the window 2018-2025: with 1 ha for loss, moving the standing minimum to 5 ha or 25 ha moves 990 or 1,207 units to another cell; with 10 ha standing, moving the loss minimum to 0.5 ha or 2 ha moves 308 or 282. A fixed number of hectares also follows the size of a unit's forest: 5 % of the forest sites with 10 to 25 ha standing have some loss, and 85 % of those with 5,000 ha or more; 349 of the 1,295 units in Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first lost under 1 % of the forest they had at the start of the window, and 74 forest sites in a cell with little or no loss recorded lost over 2 % of theirs. That is why each unit's loss is also shown as a share (below). And 173 units have under 10 ha of land and so have little or none whatever their cover: 170 of them are in Not a forest site and 3, which lost 1 ha or more in the window, in Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground. In 37 units of the cell Not a forest site standing forest covers half or more of the land. | reworded |
| about_html, block 34 | text | The tiers are provisional, to be set with the Foundation for Ecological Security: 1 Open forest, loss recorded - restore; 2 Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first; 3 Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate; 4 Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect; 5 Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground; Not a forest site is on the map and outside the list. A forester could reasonably put tier 2 above tier 1. The order is yours to change on the left; an exported list says which order made it. | The tiers are provisional, to be set with the Foundation for Ecological Security: 1 Open forest, loss recorded - restore; 2 Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first; 3 Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate; 4 Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect; 5 Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground; Not a forest site is on the map and outside the list. A forester could reasonably put tier 2 above tier 1. You can change the order on the left; an exported list says which order made it. | reworded |
| about_html, block 35 | text | Why the list is within a district and never across the state. Among the 1,649 LGD panchayat units of the agreement test's headline (at least 100 ha of land with canopy above 10 % in 2000, East Coast zone left out, 2017-2023), the two forest measures agree within districts (odds ratio 2.84, interval 1.92 to 5.24); across the state their agreement is not established (1.55, 0.80 to 3.61, an interval that includes 1). For all units of that headline together the across-state figure is 1.80, 1.10 to 2.91. So for LGD panchayat units a state-wide ranking would rest on a comparison this analysis does not establish. There is no state-wide ranking. | Why the list is within a district and never across the state. Among the 1,649 LGD panchayat units of the agreement test's headline (at least 100 ha of land with canopy above 10 % in 2000, East Coast zone left out, 2017-2023), the two forest measures agree within districts (odds ratio 2.84, interval 1.92 to 5.24); across the state their agreement is not established (1.55, 0.80 to 3.61, an interval that includes 1). For all units of that headline together the across-state figure is 1.80, 1.10 to 2.91. So for LGD panchayat units a state-wide ranking would rest on a comparison we can't establish. There is no state-wide ranking. | reworded |
| about_html, block 38 | text | Outside-village units have forest, so they are classed and listed like any unit: in the window 2018-2025, 1,804 of them are in a cell other than Not a forest site. No village plan covers them, their panel asks whose plan does, and it says what their assigned district rests on. Their census columns read no census data for this land. | Outside-village units have forest, so they are classed and listed like any unit: in the window 2018-2025, 1,804 of them are in a cell other than Not a forest site. They lie outside every revenue village, so their panel asks whose plan covers them, and says what their assigned district rests on. Their census columns read no census data for this land. | reworded |
| about_html, block 40 | text | The site-level overlay is optional, online, and off unless ticked: Hansen tree cover loss as map tiles from Global Forest Watch (World Resources Institute), for the years of the window chosen, on land with about 10 % canopy or more in 2000. Tiles: Hansen/UMD Global Forest Change v1.13, loss through 2025 - the version the unit tables are built from. It is the one exception to 'nothing is fetched from the internet': while it is ticked the page asks that provider's server for tiles, so the network address of whoever is looking, the place they are looking at and the years of the window chosen leave the machine. It can be ticked only in the decision view, and it switches itself off when that view is left or when its tiles stop arriving. Its code is in a third file, overlay.js; without that file, or without a connection, the map is exactly the offline map. It is a picture to find places by, not something to measure from. Two points of the provider's terms of use are unresolved and are the project owner's to decide: the terms ask that each request to an API carry an account's key, while the tile service's reference declares none and it returns tiles without one; and they bind a user to an attribution page written for another of the provider's services. The project's documentation (DECISION_LAYER.md, which comes with the project and not with these files) sets both out. | The loss-pixel overlay is optional, online, and off unless ticked: Hansen tree cover loss as map tiles from Global Forest Watch (World Resources Institute), for the years of the window chosen, on land with about 10 % canopy or more in 2000. Tiles: Hansen/UMD Global Forest Change v1.13, loss through 2025 - the version the unit tables are built from. It's the one thing on this page that goes online: while it's ticked, the page asks that provider's server for tiles, so your network address, the place you're looking at and the years of the window chosen leave your machine. It can be ticked only in the decision view, and it switches itself off when that view is left or when its tiles stop arriving. Its code is in a third file, overlay.js; without that file, or without a connection, the map works exactly as it does without the overlay. It is a picture to find places by, not something to measure from. Two points of the provider's terms of use are unresolved, and they are ours to decide: the terms ask that each request to an API carry an account's key, while the tile service's reference declares none and it returns tiles without one; and they bind a user to an attribution page written for another of the provider's services. The project's documentation (DECISION_LAYER.md, which comes with the project and not with these files) sets both out. | reworded |

| about_html, block 5 | text | Honest colour. More than half of the units (6,265 of 11,468) have under one hectare of forest, and a rate on a little forest is noise: between odd and even years the rank correlation of a unit's loss rate is 0.34 for units with 1 to 2 ha of forest and 0.61 at 5 to 10 ha. On the land the two measures are compared on it is still only 0.73 at 50 to 100 ha, and 0.84 at 100 to 250 ha. So on the forest layers a unit with too little of the land a layer rests on is grey - insufficient forest to assess - and never on the colour scale. The minimum is 100 ha, the floor ruled for the analysis (9,401 units grey on the Hansen layer; the coloured units still hold 97.0 % of the forest). You can set it to 50 ha or to 1 ha to see what the stricter floor hides. On the census layer the outside-village units are no census data for this land, never zero. | Small forests are greyed out. More than half of the units (6,265 of 11,468) have under one hectare of forest, and a rate on a little forest is noise: between odd and even years the rank correlation of a unit's loss rate is 0.34 for units with 1 to 2 ha of forest and 0.61 at 5 to 10 ha. On the land the two measures are compared on it is still only 0.73 at 50 to 100 ha, and 0.84 at 100 to 250 ha. So on the forest layers a unit with too little of the land a layer rests on is grey - insufficient forest to assess - and never on the colour scale. The minimum is 100 ha, the one we use in the analysis (9,401 units grey on the Hansen layer; the coloured units still hold 97.0 % of the forest). You can set it to 50 ha or 1 ha to see what the minimum hides. On the census layer the outside-village units are no census data for this land, never zero. | reworded |

| README.txt: HOW TO OPEN IT | text | Keep index.html and data.js together in one folder and open index.html in a web browser (double- click it). Nothing is installed, nothing is fetched from the internet, and it works with no connection. It was checked in Chrome and in Safari's engine on a laptop; it has not been tried on a phone. The button 'What this is, and how to read it' on the page is the full account, and 'Sources and licences' lists every source. | Open the address above in a web browser. Or keep index.html and data.js together in one folder and open index.html (double-click it): it needs nothing installed and works without a connection. Once it has loaded, the page asks no other site for anything. We've checked the map in Chrome and in Safari's engine (WebKit) on a laptop, and this copy in Chrome; it's laid out for a laptop, and in a window as narrow as a phone's the map gets no room between the two side panels, so it won't work on a phone yet (we haven't tried one). On the page, "About this map" explains how it's made and how to read it, and "Sources" says where the data comes from and under what licence. | reworded |
| README.txt: WHAT IT SHOWS | text | 11,468 units of Odisha: gram panchayat units, and the land that lies in no revenue village. For each it shows measurements of forest condition, of past MGNREGA allocation and of social need, each as a layer of its own, as they were built from public data. The 'Decision view' on the page reads the forest measurements a second way: each unit is placed in one of six cells by how much forest of 2000 has no loss recorded since (10 ha or more, or less) and by the forest loss recorded in a window of years you choose (1 ha or more, or less), and within one district the units are listed tier by tier. The six cells: Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate Open forest, loss recorded - restore Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground Not a forest site | 11,468 units covering Odisha: panchayat units, and the land outside any revenue village. For each, it shows forest condition, past MGNREGA spending and social need, each as its own layer, built from public data. The decision view reads the forest data a second way: each unit goes in one of six cells, by how much of its forest of 2000 has no loss recorded since (10 ha or more, or less) and by the forest loss recorded in a window of years you choose (1 ha or more, or less). Within one district, units are listed tier by tier. The six cells: Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate Open forest, loss recorded - restore Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground Not a forest site | reworded |
| README.txt: WHAT IT ISN'T | text | - It is not a verdict on any place and it is not a score. It is a prompt for where to look, to be set against what people who know the places see. - Standing forest in it is the forest of 2000 with no loss recorded since; not a measure of tree cover today. The page's own text gives the data provider's cautions. - The order of the tiers is provisional, to be set with the Foundation for Ecological Security: 1 Open forest, loss recorded - restore; 2 Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first; 3 Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate; 4 Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect; 5 Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground. The page lets you change it. - Which works of the official list bear on which cell is a proposal, not a ruling. - A unit is a shape on a map, not a jurisdiction; the census figures are of 2011; the geotagged record of works effectively ends in 2017-18. | - Not a verdict on any place, and not a score. It's a starting point for where to look, to be checked against what people who know the places see. - Standing forest here is the forest of 2000 with no loss recorded since; not a measure of tree cover today. The page quotes the data provider's own cautions. - The order of the tiers is provisional, to be set with the Foundation for Ecological Security: 1 Open forest, loss recorded - restore; 2 Closed forest, loss recorded - find the cause first; 3 Open forest, little or no loss recorded - assisted natural regeneration candidate; 4 Closed forest, little or no loss recorded - protect; 5 Little forest left, loss recorded - verify on the ground. You can change it on the page. - Which works from the official list fit which cell is our proposal, not a ruling. - A unit is a shape on a map, not a jurisdiction; the census figures are from 2011; the geotagged record of works effectively ends in 2017-18. | reworded |
| README.txt: LEFT OUT OF THIS COPY | text | The page has a box 'Site-level overlay'. In the full layer it can lay Hansen's loss over the map as tiles from an outside provider's server. The file that does that, overlay.js, is left out of this copy on purpose: two points of that provider's terms of use are unresolved. If the box is ticked here the page says: "The overlay's file, overlay.js, is not beside this page. Everything else on the map works as before." The page asks the network for nothing. | - The loss-pixel overlay. The full version can lay Hansen's loss over the map as tiles from an outside provider's server. We've left it out while two points of that provider's terms of use are unresolved; its box on the page is greyed out. - The development index is not in this copy. The full version also shows a 2011 village development index ('Aggregate Development Index 2011') as a layer, as a line in each unit's panel and as a column of the list. No file we share carries it. Apart from these two, this copy's two files are the full version's own. | reworded |
| README.txt: LEFT OUT OF THIS COPY | text | The full layer also shows a village development index of 2011 ('Aggregate Development Index 2011'): as a layer of its own, as a line in each unit's panel and as a column of the ranked list. The index is left out of this copy on purpose, with its value for every unit: no file that is passed on carries it. Apart from that, and from the map overlay's file, the copy's two files are the layer's own. | - The loss-pixel overlay. The full version can lay Hansen's loss over the map as tiles from an outside provider's server. We've left it out while two points of that provider's terms of use are unresolved; its box on the page is greyed out. - The development index is not in this copy. The full version also shows a 2011 village development index ('Aggregate Development Index 2011') as a layer, as a line in each unit's panel and as a column of the list. No file we share carries it. Apart from these two, this copy's two files are the full version's own. | reworded |

## 6. Figures that are not where they were

`scripts/check_site_copy.py` counts every figure and every placeholder of the old words, place by place, a label and
its "i" being one place, and holds that each stands as often in the new words. These are the differences, each
written into that check with its reason; a difference not on this list fails the check.

| Build | Place | Before | After | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the full layer | a unit's panel, section 2 | 2000: 5 time(s) | 2000: 4 time(s) | the section's heading no longer names the year: 'Loss by canopy in 2000 (what the forest mask leaves out)' became 'Loss outside the forest mask'; its rows and its 'i' still say 2000 |
| the full layer | about_html | 2000: 14 time(s) | 2000: 12 time(s) | the owner's bullet on Hansen of 11 October 2026 (his third handoff) no longer names the year: 'canopy that was fairly closed in 2000' and 'land that was already open in 2000' are gone from it |
| the full layer | about_html | 2013: 0 time(s) | 2013: 1 time(s) | the deck's item on NIRv gives the years of the test, 2013-2025 |
| the full layer | about_html | 2025: 15 time(s) | 2025: 16 time(s) | the deck's item on NIRv gives the years of the test, 2013-2025 |
| the full layer | about_html | 2026: 2 time(s) | 2026: 3 time(s) | the deck's item on NIRv gives the date of the decision, 7 October 2026 |
| the full layer | about_html | 7: 0 time(s) | 7: 1 time(s) | the deck's item on NIRv gives the date of the decision, 7 October 2026 (docs/RESULTS.md section 6) |
| the full layer | layer agreement | 2017: 3 time(s) | 2017: 2 time(s) | the deck's name is 'Hansen and CoRE Stack compared': the years left the name and still stand, twice, in the legend title |
| the full layer | layer agreement | 2023: 3 time(s) | 2023: 2 time(s) | as for 2017: the years left the layer's name and still stand in its legend title |
| the full layer | layer corestack_net | 2017: 2 time(s) | 2017: 1 time(s) | the deck's name is 'Degradation minus improvement (CoRE Stack)': the years left the name and still stand in the legend title |
| the full layer | layer corestack_net | 2023: 2 time(s) | 2023: 1 time(s) | as for 2017: the years left the layer's name and still stand in its legend title |
| the full layer | layer forest_area | 2000: 2 time(s) | 2000: 3 time(s) | the deck's name for the layer, 'Forest in 2000 (Hansen)', names the year, as its legend title and its note do; the old name was 'Area under forests' |
| the full layer | layer hansen_loss | 2001: 2 time(s) | 2001: 3 time(s) | the deck's note says 'cleared outright since 2001'; the year also stands in the layer's name and in its legend title |
| the full layer | layer hansen_loss | 2025: 2 time(s) | 2025: 1 time(s) | the deck's name is 'Tree cover loss since 2001 (Hansen)': the end year left the name and still stands in the legend title, 'Tree cover loss 2001-2025 ...' |
| the full layer | layer hansen_loss | 30: 3 time(s) | 30: 4 time(s) | the owner's words of 11 October 2026 for the layer's 'i' (his third handoff) say where the layer counts loss: 'only where canopy was above 30 % in 2000', the figure its grey notes already give |

## 8. The checks whose words changed

A check's expected words were changed only to follow a string this task reworded, and each still checks the same
fact; no expected number, count or tolerance was changed and no check was taken out. Where a caution moved into an
"i", the check now opens that "i" and reads it. Three things were tightened and one could not follow:

- the gate on the copy lets no line of the browser's log through (it let one through: "file not found" for
  `overlay.js`, which the page no longer asks for);
- `scripts/verify_decision.py` now holds that the "i" beside the order of tiers says the order is the reader's to
  change, which was in that check's name only;
- the decision view's exercise now holds that a click on the overlay's "i" does not tick its box;
- `scripts/exercise_decision_safari.py`, which reads the page's text through the Safari application and was not run,
  cannot open an "i": where it held that the overlay's note was in the page's text, it now holds that it is not.
  The note is read by `scripts/exercise_decision.py` in Chrome and in WebKit and by `scripts/verify_decision.py`.

`scripts/verify_layer.py` is unchanged: `scripts/check_site_copy.py` holds it byte for byte to the commit the stage
four checks hold it to. `scripts/exercise_layer.py` was changed only where it pinned reworded words, until
13 October 2026, when it gained the steps of the Guide button and the loader (section 14).
`scripts/exercise_layer_safari.py` pins none of them in its own lines and did not change; what it expects of a unit's
panel comes from `scripts/exercise_layer.py`.

Every block of lines that differs from the commit before the checks were changed (`2e748c9`) is a row below, for
each check and for the two document writers whose holds and quotations follow the page's words: 215 rows in
10 files. The table is `60_layer/site_copy_check_changes.csv`, where the old and the new words stand whole;
here each is cut after its first 260 characters. Since 13 October 2026 the rows of `scripts/check_site_copy.py`
itself are in the table too, against the last commit before the Guide button (`6607784`): that script did not
exist at the commit the other files are set against.

### The check of the words

`scripts/check_site_copy.py`, run 2026-10-12T23:33:18Z: 59 of 59 checks passed. In a browser it opened
99 bubbles in the full layer and 93 in the copy with the pointer, and 99 and 93 by
keyboard focus (36 and 36 of those on an "i" that the browser had first to scroll into view), in
14 and 13 states of the page. Runs of the browser part it needed: 1 for the full layer,
1 for the copy.

**One defect of the page was found by this check, and fixed.** The page closed a bubble whenever anything scrolled.
An "i" that takes keyboard focus while it is out of sight is scrolled into view by the browser, and that scroll
closed the bubble it had just opened: a reader who tabbed to an "i" below the fold of a panel saw nothing. The check
met it as a failure that came and went - it had moved the pointer onto an "i" straight after scrolling it into view,
and so won or lost the same race. The page now keeps the box with its "i" for as long as the "i" can be seen, and
closes it when the "i" is scrolled out of sight; the check gives every "i" keyboard focus, after putting it out of
sight wherever its panel can scroll; and the last of the planted errors below is the page as it was.

### The planted errors

35 of 35 errors, each planted on a copy and never on the real files, were caught by a check meant to
catch them (run 2026-10-12T23:02:24Z). The first eight are the ones the owner named; those numbered from twenty-three to
twenty-six are the four he named with the six lines, each of which puts an old line back; and those numbered
twenty-eight and twenty-nine are the two he named with the guide, a changed figure and a figure found nowhere, each
planted on a copy of the guide's text. The ones after them try the other checks of the guide and the loader.

## 10. What was seen

The pictures were taken 2026-10-12T22:35:07Z in a headless Chrome, of both builds at three window sizes, each with an "i"
opened by the pointer. Every one was opened and looked at when it was first taken, and again whenever it came out
different outside the row of the three buttons; the paragraph below says which were looked at on these builds. The
measurements are the script's; "seen" is what the picture shows. The pictures are in `60_layer/site_copy_shots/`,
outside git like the rest of that folder.

**The pictures are of the builds with the Guide button.** With the six lines, four more had been taken of each
build at the largest size: a ranked list with an East Coast badge in view, the prompt of a unit outside villages, the
comparison section's "i" in the panel of an East Coast unit, and the grid of "why this tier". On 13 October 2026
every picture was taken again, since each shows the side panel and the side panel has a third button; and more were
taken of the published copy, of the Guide's window and of the loader. Each picture was then held, pixel for pixel,
against the picture of the same name that had been kept, and looked at, before the Guide button. Of 49 pictures,
byte for byte the kept one: 1; differing only inside the row of the three buttons, and so showing everywhere
else exactly what was looked at before: 31; differing elsewhere too, each opened and looked at again:
12; new, and looked at: 5. A picture that is byte for byte the kept one keeps its note; for one that differs only inside the row of the three buttons the note stands with a sentence on the row, and the row of each such picture was cut out onto one sheet and looked at; one that differs elsewhere too was opened and looked at again, and its note says what moved; a new one was opened and looked at.

**Overall.** At 1280 x 800 and at 1000 x 700, in both builds, every bubble opened wholly inside the window and could be read: under its 'i' where there was room, above it where there was not. An open bubble lies over what is beneath it, as a bubble does; none covered its own 'i' or the label the 'i' belongs to, and no two parts of the page overlapped that did not before. Two things a reader may notice, neither of them new: a section heading as long as the unit's panel is wide leaves its 'i' alone on the next line; and the ranked list is wider than its panel at these sizes and scrolls sideways inside itself, so the 'i' of its seventh column is reached by scrolling the list. In the copy the overlay's box is plainly greyed and unticked, with the owner's sentence under it and no 'i'. The four pictures the owner asked for with his six lines, at 1280 x 800 in both builds, show his words where a reader meets them: an East Coast badge in a ranked list reads 'our placebo test fails here'; the prompt of a unit outside villages reads 'Outside every revenue village. Whose plan covers this land?'; the 'i' of the comparison's section says the placebo test is ours and fails on few districts; and the grid of 'why this tier' is headed 'Little or no forest loss in the window'. In none of them does an old line stand. With the Guide button (13 October 2026) every picture was taken again and held, pixel for pixel, against the picture kept before the button. At 1280 x 800 the three buttons stand on one line, and nothing else in any picture moved. At 1000 x 700 the side panel has no room for three on a line: Guide stands alone on a second line and the side panel's controls stand one line lower; every bubble still opens wholly inside the window, and the map, the legend and the panels to the right are as they were. The Guide's window opens at the guide's top with Close in view, shows a picture as wide as its text, and in a narrow window lets a wide table scroll inside itself. While the page opens, the map's area says 'Loading the map…' and then 'Drawing the units…' over a bar that moves and counts nothing.

**At a phone's width.** The page is laid out for a laptop, and at 390 x 844 it cannot be used. The two side panels keep most of the width between them, so the map, and the ranked list under it, are left a strip a few pixels wide. The legend, which belongs over the map, lies over the side panel and covers the names of the layers and, in the decision view, the windows. The unit's panel runs off the right edge, its figures are cut, and the page scrolls sideways. A bubble still opens wholly inside the window and can be read wherever its 'i' can be reached (the legend's, a section's in the unit's panel, the overlay's); the 'i' of the decision view's switch lies under the legend, and those of the list cannot be reached because the list has no width. Nothing was redesigned. It was not opened on a phone, and nothing was tapped. With the Guide button (13 October 2026): at 390 x 844 the row of the buttons has two lines, as it had, and Guide stands after Sources on the second, under the legend. A click on it there does not reach it; with the legend hidden by its own Hide button, the click opens the guide (measured in both builds). The Guide's window itself was looked at in a window 380 px wide: it can be read, and a table wider than it scrolls sideways inside itself.

| Build | Window | Picture | Measured | Seen |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| full layer | 1280 x 800 | the side panel, with the decision view's 'i' open | window 1280 x 800; bubble at 8, 200, 300 x 137; wholly inside the window: yes; covers its own i: no; the page scrolls sideways: no | The bubble opens under the 'i' beside 'Decision view', wholly inside the window, white on black and easy to read. While it is open it lies over the first names of the list of layers beneath it, as a bubble does; it covers neither its own 'i' nor its label. The side panel, the map with its legend at the lower right and the empty unit panel do not overlap one another. Taken again with the Guide button (13 October 2026). It differs from the picture kept before the button only inside the row of the buttons, where Guide now stands after About this map and Sources, the three on one line. Outside that row no pixel differs, so everywhere else it is the picture this note was written of. |
| full layer | 1280 x 800 | the legend, with its 'i' open | window 1280 x 800; bubble at 580, 530, 300 x 102; wholly inside the window: yes; covers its own i: no; the page scrolls sideways: no | The legend's 'i' stands after the legend's title. Its bubble opens under it, within the legend's width and wholly inside the window, readable; while open it lies over the legend's first class rows. Taken again with the owner's six lines: the bubble now holds his words for this layer - it counts loss only where canopy was above 30 % in 2000, the next layer shows it on more open land, and what Hansen can't see is thinning under a canopy that's still standing. Nothing in it speaks of land that was open in 2000. Taken again with the Guide button (13 October 2026). It differs from the picture kept before the button only inside the row of the buttons, where Guide now stands after About this map and Sources, the three on one line. Outside that row no pixel differs, so everywhere else it is the picture this note was written of. |
| full layer | 1280 x 800 | a unit's panel, with a section's 'i' open | window 1280 x 800; bubble at 798, 537, 300 x 242; wholly inside the window: yes; covers its own i: no; the page scrolls sideways: no | The unit's panel is scrolled just far enough for the 'i' of 'Hansen and CoRE Stack compared (2017-2023)' to show at its foot. (the deck's words not used; the owner may prefer to add the missing rows) | fixed (Step 1, commit 2e748c9) |

Four things of the handoff were not done exactly as written:

- the picture "the decision view's list, with a district chosen and the prompt's i open" is two pictures, because
  the prompt stands only while no district is chosen (section 10);
- the sentence the owner gave for the scripts that were called unchanged is said of `scripts/exercise_layer.py`
  alone, with a clause for `scripts/exercise_layer_safari.py`, which did not need a change (section 8);
- the check of the words gained one group the owner did not list, on the checks' own changes (its checks `e1` and
  `e2`), so that "no check is removed or loosened" can be read off a table; and in a browser it gives every "i"
  keyboard focus as well as the pointer, which is how the defect of section 9 was pinned down;
- this document is written from the `60_layer/site_copy_*` files, as asked, and from what its section on the runs
  needs besides: the runner's record, the record of the gate on the copy and the two builds' own records. It also
  reads four documents, to hold a fix or a list against each (section 14).

## 13. The six lines of the owner's third handoff

Later on 11 October 2026 the owner ruled on six lines that section 3 then listed for him. They were untrue or
misleading, he gave his words for each, and he asked that every other string stay as it is.

- **It is our placebo test, not "CoRE Stack's own check".** The check that fails in the East Coast zone is this
  project's: it uses CoRE Stack's Improvement class as a check on its Degradation class. Its failure rests on few
  districts, and the two "i" that speak of it now say so.
- **Hansen does see loss on open land; what it cannot see is thinning.** The layer after the Hansen layer draws
  Hansen's loss on land that was open in the first year of its record.
- **"Little or no" loss, as the cells' names say.** The column holds every unit under the loss minimum in the
  window, not only units with none. Only what the page prints changes; the decision table's code for the column is
  as it was.
- **Outside villages: the question is kept and the claim dropped.** Nothing in the repository shows that this land
  is in no village plan, and works are geotagged on it.
- **The Hansen row of Sources says what was reconciled**, and up to which year. The copy's README takes its credits
  from that row and follows it.
- **Block rasters, not a dashboard**, as `docs/LAYER.md` says of the same thing.

**His ruling on the prompt.** The prompt of an outside-village unit comes from the decision table,
`55_decision/condition_by_unit.csv`, column `governance_prompt`. To rebuild that table for one sentence would send
the fourth stage through four more steps, so it is not rebuilt now. Until it is next rebuilt the builder of the
decision view, `scripts/_layer_decision.py`, shows the new words in place of the table's, a change of the page only.
The builder still guards the table: it stops unless the table's prompt is exactly the old sentence, so that the swap
cannot hide a change in the table. When the decision table is next rebuilt, with the Foundation's ruling on the
tiers, `scripts/05_build_decision.py` takes the new words and the swap goes. `scripts/verify_decision.py` holds both
halves: that the page shows the new words, and that the table still carries the old sentence.

**Every string that differs from the builds kept before the six lines.** Both builds were copied aside before a
line was changed, as they were before the words were first rewritten. `scripts/check_site_copy.py` holds the builds
on disk against those copies (its group `f`): no string was dropped, and none was added but the four that the Guide
button and the loader put on the page on 13 October 2026 (section 14); each string below differs by exactly the
owner's replacement and by nothing else; no place of a fix still holds its old words; no other part of `data.js`
differs but the build's own time and the guide's key; and `index.html` differs only in the places the Guide button
and the loader changed, each written out in the check. In the full layer 20 strings differ, in the
copy 21. Where the same string differs in both builds it is one row. A long string is shown by the stretch
that differs and what stands on either side of it, with a mark where it is cut; the record of the check holds each
whole.

counted as a space, in every file of text git tracks but the registered inputs and the documents this task writes by
script (those say what changed, and so quote the old words). None of these is a string of the page. They are of three kinds: a check or a builder that names an old line in order to forbid it, to
guard the decision table's sentence or to record the change; the script that builds the decision table, which keeps
its sentence until that table is next rebuilt; and a hand-kept document or a stage script's own account, which this
handoff does not rewrite. `README.md` speaks of Hansen on the forest mask, which is blind to open land by
construction: the owner asked that it be left.

## 14. The Guide button and the loader (13 October 2026)

On 13 October 2026 the owner asked for three things: a third button on the page, Guide, which opens his guide to the
map with its pictures; a loader, which says what the page is doing while it opens; and the site published with them.
Nothing that section 2 to section 13 describe was changed by it, and no figure, class, count or colour of the page
moved: the checks of section 9 still hold both builds against the copies kept before, with what was added written
out in the check itself.

**What was added to the page.** These strings, and no other, are new in `index.html`, the same in both builds:

| Where | The string |
|---|---|
| index.html, <button> | Guide |
| index.html, title= | What every control does, and how the map was made and checked |
| index.html, markup | Loading the map… |
| index.html, a string of its script | Drawing the units… |

The button stands after About this map and Sources and opens the guide in the window those two use; Close, or a
click outside, shuts it; an address that ends in `#open=guide` opens it. `index.html` changed in 10 places, each
written out in `scripts/check_site_copy.py`; with every one put back, the page is byte for byte the page kept before.
Beyond the button and the loader themselves, two small things are among them: each of the three texts now opens at its
top, and the three buttons have a little less padding, so that they stay on one line beside a scroll bar in a window
1280 px wide. In one 1000 px wide the side panel has no room for three, and Guide stands on a second line,
under the other two. At a phone's width the legend lies over it (section 10).

**The guide.** `docs/GUIDE.md` is the owner's text (36,840 bytes) and the one source of that window.
`scripts/_guide.py`, a converter of its own for what the guide uses, with no package, turns it into HTML when the
layer is built; a tag of the source is never passed through. The HTML is carried in `data.js` as one more key,
`guide_html`, beside `about_html` and `sources_html`: 923,633 bytes, of which 880,924 are its pictures. A link
in it opens only when clicked, in a new tab, and nothing else in it carries an address. The copy carries the same
guide, unchanged: the guide describes the published copy, and the full layer shows that same text.

**Its pictures.** 5 pictures of the published copy, in a window of 1280 × 800, taken 2026-10-12T21:05:25Z in
headless Chrome 154.0.8037.99 and captured by the browser itself as WebP at quality 80; no note is open but the one the last
picture asks for. Each goes into the guide's HTML as its own bytes, so the window opens without a connection; the
build stops if one the guide names is missing. They could not be in the build before they existed, so they were taken
of a build made with the guide's picture lines left out. None of them shows the Guide's window, so that build looks
the same; and taken again of the builds on disk (2026-10-12T23:32:48Z), each came out byte for byte the picture in
`docs/guide/`. Each was opened and looked at. "Its caption" is the guide's own line under the picture.

| Picture | Bytes | What was done | Its caption | Seen |

**The loader.** While the page opens, the map's area says "Loading the map…" until `data.js` has arrived, then
"Drawing the units…", and the message goes when the map is ready. The first message is plain markup placed before the tag that
asks for `data.js`; once the data is in, the page changes the message, gives the browser a frame to paint it, and only
then draws the units. Its bar moves and counts nothing: `scripts/verify_layer.py`, which is unedited, holds that the
page's script never uses `fetch` or `XMLHttpRequest`, and without them a browser reports no progress for `data.js`. If
the data never arrives, the page says so as it did. What a reader sees while the page loads was looked at in two of
the frames the browser painted (the last two pictures of section 10).

**The sizes.** In `60_layer/` the two files were 22,500,277 bytes together before the guide (`index.html` 210,942, `data.js`
22,289,335; the layer built 2026-10-11T15:01:22Z) and are 23,427,611 bytes with it (`index.html` 214,465, `data.js` 23,213,146), against the 25,000,000
bytes that `scripts/verify_layer.py` allows. The guide's pictures are 660,688 bytes as files.

**What the checks gained.** No check was taken out, and no expected number or count was changed; counts rose only by
what this handoff added, and every changed block is a row of section 8. The layer's exercise now ends, in Chrome,
"60 of 60 control actions did what they should; 29 units clicked on the map (0 clicks landed on a neighbour, which was then the unit compared); 1276 of 1276 displayed values equal the stage tables; script errors: none" and, in WebKit, "60 of 60 control actions did what they should; 29 units clicked on the map (0 clicks landed on a neighbour, which was then the unit compared); 1276 of 1276 displayed values equal the stage tables; script errors: none"; the gate on the copy has 9 steps. `scripts/check_site_copy.py` gained a
group of its own, `g`, and three of its checks that held "nothing else was added" were widened by exactly what was
added, each written out: `a2` by the one key; `f1` by the strings above, all of which must be there; `f2` by the
places of `index.html`, each found exactly once.

| g1 | the guide's HTML held to docs/GUIDE.md without the converter, in both builds: with the marks of Markdown taken out of the one and the tags out of the other, the two read the same, character for character but for spacing; the HTML has no tag and no attribute but the few the converter writes, and no script; its links are the guide's own, in order, each an https address opened in a new tab; nothing else in it carries an address; and it holds the 5 pictures the guide names, in the guide's order, each as the bytes of its file under docs/guide/, with the guide's words for it, at 1280 x 800 | 28,143 characters of words; 11 links; 5 pictures |
| g2 | the Guide button and the loader, in the index.html of both builds, read as text: the button stands once, right after About this map and Sources, with the owner's label and hover text; the button and #open=guide both open `guide_html` in the window the other two texts use; 'Loading the map...' is plain markup in the map's area, before the tag that asks for data.js; 'Drawing the units...' is set once the data is in, after the message for data that did not load, and the browser is given a frame to paint it before the map is drawn; the loader is put away in the line before the page says it is ready; its bar only moves, and nothing sets its width; and its two messages have no figure, no '?' or ';', at most six words, and no phrase of the lists. (What a browser then does is scripts/exercise_layer.py's to show.) | 'Loading the map…' and 'Drawing the units…', in both builds |
| g3 | the guide's words, as both builds carry them: none of the phrases the deck lists, none of the eight the owner added with his six lines, and not 'is not beside this page'; no finding of the audit that is marked fixed; no block that opens 'This shows'; and the development index is named nowhere in it - not by the words in any case, not by its key, not by the name of its layer | 27 phrases and 42 fixed findings looked for in 230 blocks of the guide |
| g4 | every figure of docs/GUIDE.md is traced. The guide is cut into sentences and table cells; for each one that holds a figure, all its figures must stand together, each written the same way, in one passage of one of the named files (the page's own strings in data.js, docs/GAP.md, docs/DECISION_LAYER.md, README.md, the records of the runner, of the copy's check and of the pictures looked at, and the provider's cautions), and that passage must share a word with the sentence; for a cell that is only a figure it must hold another cell of the same row, and for a row about one file it must name the file. Allowed, and nothing else: thousands separators, a space before %, the minus sign, and x for the multiplication sign. A lone small figure can stand, beside a shared word, in a passage about something else; every trace is in 60_layer/site_copy_guide_figures.csv to be read.) | 180 sentences and cells with a figure, in 194 rows of the table (a sentence that draws on two passages has two): 178 found in one passage, 10 that come from a figure of a named file by a listed rule, 6 counts of this build's own runs; 2 sentences listed as standing in two passages |

**The gate on the guide's figures** (check `g4`). The guide is cut into sentences and table cells. For each one that
holds a figure, all its figures must stand together, each written the same way, in one passage of one of the files
the owner named, a passage that shares a word with the sentence. Allowed, and nothing else: thousands separators, a
space before the per cent sign, the minus sign, and the letter for the multiplication sign. 180 sentences and
cells hold a figure. Their traces are 194 rows of a table, since a sentence that draws on two passages has two:
178 found so, 10 figures that come from a figure of a named file by a rule written out in the check, and
6 counts of this build's own runs, which other checks of the group hold to the records of those runs. Every
trace is in `60_layer/site_copy_guide_figures.csv`, with the passage beside the sentence.

The files: the page's own strings in data.js; `docs/GAP.md`; `docs/DECISION_LAYER.md`; `README.md`; `60_layer/decision_runs.json`; `70_share/exercise_share_chrome.json`; `00_raw/05_forest_reference/provider_cautions.json`; `60_layer/site_copy_looked_at.json`; `00_raw/05_forest_reference/umd_global_forest_change_2025_v1_13_page_2026-10-08.html`. The last is not among the files the owner named. It is the registered page of the University of
Maryland, which the record of the provider's cautions names as its input, and it is used for one sentence only: the
citation of the paper behind Hansen's data. The record this check itself writes was among the files he named; only
the count of its own checks is held to it, and to the run that writes it.

**What the gate does not show.** That a passage says what the sentence says. A sentence with several figures, or
with a large one, is held tightly, because another passage would have to hold them all. A lone small figure can stand
beside a shared word in a passage about something else. Every trace was read once, by eye, on 13 October 2026; where
the passage found for a lone figure was not the right one, the right one is named in the check.

**Every figure that stands in no named file as the guide writes it**, with how it comes from one that does. The gate
